mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i am very pleased that this text has been adopted by the committee on employment. the vote on this report, which is now being negotiated on the union's 2011 budget, is timely. parliament has expressed its willingness to make youth policies a priority. we welcome this, but these policies cannot be limited to the mobility of young people. they must also concern their rights. that's exactly what this is about. the abuses in the employment of trainees are worrying and risk creating a parallel labour market for young people. the precarious situation of young trainees, who repeat their traineeships where they could be employed, is detrimental to the european economy and particularly to the financing of social security systems. in this respect, i am pleased that my amendment calling for decent working and living conditions for these trainees has been incorporated into this report. in my view, this report is a step towards a real european status for the trainee.
in writing. a regulation which affects the daily lives of european citizens and yet is still awaiting adoption, the council's position being considered unambitious and unacceptable by the european parliament. like other modes of transport, bus and coach passengers have essential rights which we are trying to guarantee in this regulation: compensation in the event of delay, cancellation, loss or damage to luggage, non-discrimination for disabled and reduced mobility passengers, and above all the responsibility of bus drivers in the event of injury or death. travel has become much more democratic in recent years, and this is to be welcomed. but we cannot sell out prices at the expense of the safety of the hundreds of thousands of children, elderly people and families who travel our european roads every year. the adoption of this regulation is therefore also intended to put an end to the unscrupulous practices of companies offering attractive offers to customers who often have no other choice for travel.
in writing. in an ideal world, youth unemployment would be illegal. the reality is quite different: 5.5 million europeans under 25 are unemployed, unpaid, living in uncertainty and doubt. the united kingdom, spain and france are the countries most affected, but the situation is not very different in belgium. since 2000, youth unemployment in brussels has never been below 30%. the fault lies in early school drop-out, lack of language proficiency, lack of partnerships between schools and businesses. this is why, despite its limited powers in the field of social policy, the european union must promote access for young people to the labour market. the turunen report is in line with this. he outlines innovative ways of tackling youth unemployment. the new recommendation for a 'european quality charter for traineeships' to avoid the trivialization of the traineeship is part of this logic. it is accompanied by the ambitious idea of guaranteeing training to every young person who has been unemployed for more than four months. it also advocates a "decent income" for young people in employment, whether they have a degree or not. maybe it's because you always remember your first paycheck.
in writing. - a total of 5.5 million young people, or 21.4% of all young people under 25, are unemployed today. that's almost twice the average unemployment rate. in the european year of combating poverty and exclusion, the practical proposals of mr turunen are not a day too soon. we must prevent a whole generation of young people from being socially excluded and from jeopardizing europe's economic and social future. young people should have the right to a job or paid traineeship, training or a combination of both after 4 months of unemployment. the traineeship must provide a reasonable income and be limited in time, through a quality charter. the resolution also calls for 10% of the european social fund to be reserved for projects helping young people to find work. the european commission must also ensure that existing legislation in the various member states does not discriminate against young people and guarantees their access to social benefits in employment. long-term unemployment at a young age is detrimental to self-confidence and a positive outlook for the future. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this problem.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of the turunen report on promoting access for young people to the labour market, strengthening the status of trainees, apprentices and traineeships, which proposes ambitious but nonetheless concrete and realistic measures to combat youth unemployment. with 21.4% of young europeans under 25 unemployed, it is high time for action. we would therefore like to introduce a 'european youth guarantee' to ensure that young people are unemployed for no more than four months and to ensure that they have quality training and a decent income. i would particularly stress the need to provide young people with quality training to avoid the exploitation which exists in many companies and administrations. trainees are not volunteers and cannot replace an employee; they must be provided with educational support and minimum benefits.
in writing. − people today work in all kinds of capacities: employed or (apparently) self-employed, full-time, part-time, limited or unlimited, working from home or on a temporary basis. the fact is that the flexibility demanded by employers often comes at the expense of the necessary protection of workers. the green group today tabled an alternative resolution which sought to focus on a clear statute for 'workers' (in any capacity) and called for a directive guaranteeing equal rights for all workers. this resolution has not succeeded and parliament has missed an opportunity to build a stronger social europe. however, i also supported the original resolution because it calls for the extension of workers' protection, the guarantee of workers' basic rights, safe transition to other work and the strengthening of the role of trade unions. in the european year for the eradication of poverty, we can certainly use this approach, especially when we know that more and more people are employed in atypical contracts. in europe, 8% of the working population is poor and 17% of europeans have low incomes. action is therefore certainly desirable and i therefore welcome the position of the whole european parliament.
on behalf of the alde group. mr president, i am satisfied with the presentation of the package by the president of the commission and, if i may be permitted to give an opinion, i suggest that you table it as soon as possible, on the basis of your right of initiative. put it on the council's table and put it on the european parliament's table because that is the mechanism of the union. the commission has the initiative. i therefore wish the working group that has been set up much success, but in the end the commission will have to take up its responsibilities and put a proposal on the table of the various institutions. nevertheless, mr president, there are still some questions about this package. the first and fundamental question is who will manage this economic governance. are they the member states, the council or the community institutions, i.e. the european central bank and the european commission? the european parliament is quite clear: it must be the community institutions, because i have never seen that the member states could exercise this control themselves. it's not working. it never worked in the past, and it won't work in the future either. secondly, i am in favour of sanctions, but not only in relation to the stability pact, because, as you said, this is only one aspect of the policy. it is also necessary to do so, for example, at the level of competitiveness, structural reforms and the european economy. there must be sanctions and i ask some member states to be more consistent on this point. they defend - they even demand - sanctions in relation to the stability pact, but when it comes to sanctions at the level of europe 2020, in terms of competitiveness, they are much more reluctant. it's either one or the other. if we want sanctions, they must be applied in all areas. my third point is that we must also think, mr president of the commission, about a strategy for economic growth. we cannot just say we are going to reduce the deficit. this reduction is necessary - as a former budget minister, i like to reduce deficits - but alongside this, we also need a policy of economic growth, and the only body that can fuel this is the european union. the union has borrowing capacity, it has projects, at the level of the pan-european infrastructure that is needed, as mr monti's report emphasizes, and it is therefore in this sense also that the commission must make proposals: not only to reduce deficits - which is necessary because the stability pact requires it - but also to develop a credible strategy for economic growth, so as not to fall into the trap of economic stagnation in the japanese style, such as we have known for more than ten years. the fourth element is stress testing. i'm glad the decision was made to make them public. it should have been done a year ago, as the americans did, because this is the only way to replenish the credits at the level of the private sector. finally - and i will end with this, mr president - on financial supervision, let us be very clear on this point: there will be no agreement with parliament if the council and the member states do not respect the fact that in the event of conflict, it is the european authority that has the last word. that's a crucial point. moreover, this element is included in the commission's proposals. this is the starting point for the larosière exercise. the member states have not yet understood. it is time, mr president, for the member states to accept this. otherwise there will be no agreement on financial supervision.

the rapporteur. - madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to thank the shadow rapporteurs for the good cooperation which, in my view, has enabled us to adopt a coherent set of positions and recommendations. the financial crisis has shown that the remuneration policy in the financial sector was too focused on short-term profits and that this encouraged risk-taking behaviour with all its consequences for the whole economy after the banking crisis. moreover, it has been shown that non-binding recommendations on remuneration policy have only a limited effect and it is therefore important that we establish rules of the game which should contribute to sustainable growth of the companies concerned and the economy as a whole. the political agreement on the capital requirements directive proposed so justly by my colleague arlene mccarthy is an important step forward, as it contains for the first time binding provisions on the structure and payment of bonuses in the financial sector. in particular, the limitation of cash payments, the provision that in the event of poor performance the bonus should also be limited and the spread of the payment of bonuses over several years seem to me to be important points. in my report i have tried to show that this is not enough and that we must look at the remuneration policy from a broader perspective and strengthen the so-called corporate governance rules so that the rules of the game and the internal procedures for all companies in the financial sector on the one hand and for listed companies on the other meet the same criteria. first, it is important to establish and develop sound internal and external controls over remuneration policy. companies should therefore have a remuneration committee which is independent and accountable to shareholders and supervisors, who should also have access to all relevant information. the committee should also be able to work closely with the risk committee in assessing incentives created by the reward system. people involved in risk control must, of course, be independent of the business units they control, have sufficient authority and be rewarded, regardless of the performance of the business units they supervise. risk management arrangements should be subject to detailed scrutiny by the supervisor and companies should establish an internal procedure to deal with problems and disputes that may arise between risk management and their operational units. the remuneration itself should reflect as much as possible the long-term performance of the company. a bonus cannot be guaranteed without a fight. the size of the variable remuneration should be based on predetermined and measurable performance criteria and should not be based only on quantitative but also on qualitative criteria. moreover, from the point of view of social justice and for ethical reasons, the difference between the highest and lowest remuneration in a company must remain reasonable. the payment of part of the variable remuneration must be made over a sufficiently long period. moreover, more than half should be paid out through shares or share-based instruments and it should be possible to effectively recover part of those bonuses. there should also be a ceiling on the amount of severance pay, limited to a maximum of two years' salary, and it should certainly not be paid in the event of a failure to perform or voluntary departure. in the interests of transparency, it is important that details of the company pension and supplementary pension schemes are made public and that shareholders should be able to express their views on the remuneration policy of their company. we also call for the development of an international structure to make public which people earn more than 1 million euros, including the main elements of salary bonuses, long-term bonuses and pension contributions. we ask the european commission to continue its work and to develop strict binding principles on remuneration in the financial sector in addition to those we have already agreed. for listed companies we ask that a system be set up which will enable us to know in all transparency which companies are keeping their agreements and which are not. i think we can go further on the subject. i look forward to your proposals on corporate governance, commissioner, which will be announced in the green paper, because that is also a dossier on which we will certainly continue to work in the coming months and years.
the rapporteur. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i too want to be brief. i think we all agree that there are rules of the game in terms of remuneration policy and it is clear - as the evaluation of the recommendations on remuneration policy published by the commission a year ago shows - that they should be as binding as possible. i am aware, of course, that not everyone wants to go as far as they do and that not everyone is as ambitious, but we must all agree that what we are discussing today and hopefully approving tomorrow is only a first step in the right direction. but it must not be the end of the road. obviously, there's still some work to be done. the role of supervisors should be strengthened to enable external control of the remuneration policy in the enterprises. the role of the remuneration committees should be clarified, as well as the link with the risk management people. the role of shareholders must also be strengthened. i am therefore also very pleased to hear, commissioner, that you are in favour of a regulatory framework on corporate governance for all companies and i understand that this will be the subject of a thorough debate with the stakeholders in the coming months. this is important and it is also an important signal to our citizens that we are committed to establishing good rules of the game here in the interests of the companies themselves and in the interests of the economy.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we were elected in belgium in the middle of the food crisis, the chicken crisis with pcbs and such issues. one of the first things the commission proposed was the white paper on food safety, which i think proposed 83 measures to ensure food safety in the european union. so many years later, 11 years in between, we can be proud of the work we've done. i think that about 90% of all that work has been done and one of the few remaining issues is indeed related to the announcement at the time by the european commission to look at how we should deal with these kinds of products, with novel foods. foodstuffs produced using new production techniques which did not exist before 15 may 1997. the commission's original proposal of january 2008 was a proposal that sought to achieve a number of objectives: the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. i think that mrs liotard has had a brilliant career as rapporteur over the last three years, working well with the shadow rapporteurs and working well within the committee on the environment, public health and food safety of the european parliament. it succeeded in securing a very large majority for its proposals at first reading, a majority which is rarely achieved. it was 658 mps who supported her work. so i think it is important, commissioner, and the council, which is unfortunately absent, to know that this parliament is one and incredibly strong. we believe that foodstuffs derived from cloned animals and their offspring our presidency. the first challenge for europe is of course to get back to sustainable economic growth. this return naturally requires a strengthening of financial stability. we must restore financial present yesterday. in any event, the commission has also recently announced an overall package of 20 other proposals in the area of financial services. ladies and gentlemen, the belgian presidency will try, in close consultation with you, with parliament, to draw up a roadmap to ensure that they are implemented by the end of 2011. on the other hand, as we know, the task force will be ready in october with its recommendations on strengthening budgetary discipline, the stability and growth pact and economic governance. the recent euro crisis has shown that a monetary union without genuine economic coordination is unworkable. the union needs economic governance more than ever. the task force will therefore make recommendations for more rigorous monitoring, particularly at the budgetary level. this monitoring will not only concern the budgetary policies of our member states, but also the economic parameters which allow us to judge the credibility of the budgets presented. the sustainable economic growth. in the era of globalisation, we need to step up our efforts to make europe a competitive and innovative economy again, and to become a low-carbon economy. the way forward is the eu 2020 strategy for innovation and jobs. the belgian presidency will build on the flagship initiatives announced by the commission. but sustainable growth is not just a matter of economic parameters. it is also, and above all, a matter of human persons. it is judged by what it brings to the greatest number. and as we can see in many countries, there is no sustainable growth, no real sustainable growth without social cohesion, which is the second major european challenge and the second priority of our presidency. this is why belgium insisted and won in the eu 2020 programme that the objective of fighting poverty be extended to the broader notion of social cohesion. whether for that reason or to promote economic growth, ladies and gentlemen, we must put europe back to work, we must raise the employment rate. ladies and gentlemen, i would like to dwell for a moment on a subject which is very close to my heart: that of our industrial base, the industrial base of the european economy. it seems to me that europe today is in fact sometimes lacking in joint industrial projects, emblematic and inspiring projects, as were and are, for example, ariane, high-speed trains or airbus. these are not prestige, symbols, but real levers for our prosperity and the creation of future jobs. recently, i have been struck by economic analyses that show that countries with a strong industrial base have been better able to withstand the crisis than those with a more service-oriented economy. the european industrial fabric must be renewed. there are not necessarily traditional manufacturing sectors that are doomed either to relocate to low-wage countries or to disappear. but there are modes of production that are. through innovation, through the production of high-quality products with high added value, european countries are already succeeding in safeguarding a high-performing, profitable industry with employees who are among the best paid in the world. ladies and gentlemen, we europeans have won the telecommunications revolution. we have not been as successful in computer science. we certainly must not miss the green revolution, not only research, but also, and most importantly, the production of green technology. in fact, europe has excellent researchers. but the united states and china, for example, are catching up to us when it comes to turning research results into industrial production. the commission's flagship initiative in the industrial field will therefore be particularly awaited. we also attach the utmost importance to the december council, which will be devoted to innovation in industry. the chapter on growth and innovation is a concrete issue which we want to unblock under our presidency. it is the european patent we have been waiting for for thirty years. we must think of our enterprises, especially smes. how much money and energy is being wasted by our companies for the lack of a single procedure at the level of the 27! importance to the citizens in concrete cases. now we must put words into action. i believe that the credibility of european innovation policy is at stake. ladies and gentlemen, the protection of the environment and the climate is the third great challenge for which europe, for which the european union stands, is at once the third objective of our presidency. there is no real sustainable economic growth without protecting our planet - a planet that we do not own but that we borrow from our children. the european union is worldleader in measures to protect the environment and to combat greenhouse gas emissions. the european union is worldleader in reducing its greenhouse gas emissions. but the role of the forerunners is not enough. the eu is not satisfied with the way the un climate heren, ik verwees er al naar. i'm going to the beach. the european union is like people who say they have everything to be happy. indeed, the european union is very attractive. the european union has its welfare, its system of solidarity and its very human democratic form of government. they also tend to be right, many people on other continents are, people in europe want a better life. indeed, the overwhelming majority came yesterday with good intentions, looking for what was recently said during a conference on afghanistan that would be called "the modest miracle of a normal life". such immigration was, is and will be a source of enrichment for our continent. on the other hand, others today also abuse the system of freedom. the european union is a major player in the fight against organised crime. the european union is not a terrorist organization. ladies and gentlemen, the security of our citizens, the guarantee of their security and freedom is therefore a fourth great challenge for europe. the union must become more than a space of justice, security and freedom. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the stockholm programme to include the following areas: the development of a community policy on migration, asylum and the fight against terrorism is of particular importance for belgium. in the field of criminal law cooperation, our priority is the recognition of the courts of the member states. the commission has also paid particular attention to the development of a european system for the procurement of evidence. in connection with the fight glad that we can come to an agreement tomorrow. the belgian presidency builds on the highly appreciated work of the spanish presidencies in this area. this brings me, last but certainly not least, to the fifth challenge facing the union: its role on the international scene. the challenges there are huge and numerous: combating climate change; helping to preserve or restore peace; promoting the spread of open government; combating international terrorism; preventing the spread of weapons of mass destruction; ending gross violations of human rights in areas of conflict, with special attention to violence against women; drawing up a coherent common energy policy; preserving and improving our market share. as already mentioned, the copenhagen summit painfully demonstrated that being the world's largest trading bloc and being rich and attractive and a self-proclaimed role model of soft power does not buy us sufficient international bargaining power. the union has to develop more leverage to bring others on board and create coalitions for the realization of its objectives for peace, for sustainable development, for security and for free trade that is also fair. this requires more than ever that the union speaks with one voice, and preferably with one mouth. the lisbon treaty provides the union with new tools for this purpose. in the next six months, the union will start the establishment of a european diplomatic service. this is also one of the major challenges of our presidency. we have indeed to decide on the organisation and operation of the external action service, and we have to adopt, with parliament, the financial and staff regulations of that service. belgium will do everything possible for a smooth and successful start to this external action service, which will enhance the visibility and also the credibility of the european union worldwide. enlargement is another major element. the negotiations with candidate states have to be pursued vigorously, and all candidates must be judged solely on their own merits, in accordance with the renewed consensus of the european council of december 2006. the negotiations with croatia could enter their final phase under our presidency. with turkey, they will continue their race. with iceland, they will start. for the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, the presidency will endeavour to find an agreement for starting the accession negotiations. our belgian presidency is in fact the first one which takes place entirely under the lisbon treaty. belgium feels honour bound to exercise it with full respect for the letter and the spirit of the treaty, so as to create the right precedents. you are of course aware that elections have taken place in my country, that the negotiations on the formation of a new coalition will take some time, and that my government is a so-called caretaker government. i would like to assure you that this will not in any way diminish the vigour and enthusiasm of our presidency. this 12th presidency is supported by a broad consensus in my country and by a long european tradition. we have in our political world in belgium, at the federal as well close cooperation with the other member states of the trio, and with the european institutions. moreover, the government is supported by the european conviction of the population. the nine political parties taking part in belgium in the federal and regional governments represent 90% of the population of the country. belgium is one of the countries where the european idea and the project of european integration still command very wide popular support. last but not least, we have carefully and deliberately embedded our presidency in the european agenda so as to set realistic goals and to promote them efficiently. i do not doubt our ability to conduct our presidency efficiently, just as i do not doubt that the new government, when it is formed, will continue it smoothly and seamlessly. as i made clear, a rotating presidency is part of an ongoing european process; it does not constitute an entirely new start. however, each presidency brings its own accents and the start is always an opportunity to refocus the attention of the population on the importance of the european union, on the importance of european ideals. the festivities which surround the start of our presidency enthusiastic part in celebrating the start of our 12th presidency. i also want to express my sincere appreciation for the meetings i was able to have with colleagues in these last days, with you, with of the european citizens. i would like to address a last word to those populations whom you represent. i would indeed like to urge them to believe in themselves, to have ambition for themselves, for their countries and for europe. our european union must avoid two pitfalls: the pitfall of despondency and the pitfall of complacency. there is no reason for despondency. we should not fear the new competition in an increasingly multi-polar world. on the contrary, we should rejoice in it. for we ourselves helped to bring this new world about, for example by our efforts in favour of a more equitable distribution of resources and power. as has been said, what is happening now is not "the decline of the west" but "the rise of the rest". we must throw ourselves into this competition with zest and confidence confidence in ourselves and in all the assets we have. nor are there grounds for complacency. we can certainly be proud of great achievements in the past. we have systems of government which combine political and economic freedom with social solidarity and ecological responsibility. we offer many of our citizens a living standard which is amongst the highest in the world. but we can't just sit back and enjoy what we have. the rest of the world doesn't owe us a living. we have to work for it; we have to work even harder for it. i do not think that this assembly needs developing its diplomatic service. i stand before you, the representatives of almost half a billion european citizens, to ask you for your cooperation and your support for those ambitions of our presidency. the treaty of lisbon has, i am happy to say, strengthened the powers of the european parliament and thus made it more than ever the driving force for european integration. my colleagues the commission is also responsible for the implementation of the budget. - mr president, mr prime minister, ladies and gentlemen, belgium is taking over the rotating presidency of the council at a key moment for the european union, and i thank the prime minister for presenting such a clear and ambitious programme. the challenges of the coming months are very clear. these include putting in place new mechanisms to coordinate our economic policies, stabilising the economic recovery through the implementation of the europe 2020 strategy for growth and jobs, and ensuring that europe shoulders its global responsibilities. the june european council supported the proposals put forward by the commission in its communication of 12 may on strengthening fiscal discipline and macroeconomic surveillance, which were also discussed in the context of the task force chaired by mr van rompuy. the final report of the task force will be presented by october. in the meantime, the commission has stepped up its work to translate these guidelines into concrete proposals, notably in its communication of 30 june. the aim is to strengthen macroeconomic surveillance, including through sanctions and warning mechanisms, and the stability and growth pact, with a particular focus on the issue of the level of debt and deficits. finally, the european semester will make it possible to achieve more effective ex ante coordination of policies. it is crucial that this is completed as quickly as possible, and commissioner olli rehn is making every effort to bring the necessary legislative proposals forward as soon as possible. i am counting on the belgian presidency, as well as on the european parliament, to move forward with the work so that we have a new system in place by the end of 2010. the belgian semester should allow decisive progress in the implementation of the europe 2020 strategy. this implies that each member state should adopt this strategy and integrate it into the various policies: finance, budget, employment, innovation, environment, energy, education and social policy. the instrument for achieving this is the preparation by each member state of its national reform programme in the coming months. the follow-up of this exercise by all the institutions is a prerequisite for the success of the strategy. the various community policies prepared by the commission's collegiate work will support and amplify the movement which will be initiated by each member state. the flagship initiatives that the commission will present by the end of the year will be crucial in coordination of commissioners andor and vassiliou, will aim to create the conditions for the modernisation of the labour market with the aim of improving employment rates and ensuring the sustainability of our social models. to create jobs, we need growth, and much of our future growth will have to come from innovation. the flagship initiative 'a union for innovation', coordinated by european innovation partnerships to address societal challenges in areas where the european union can bring added value, and to improve the efficiency of european, national and regional innovation systems. it is particularly important to make progress on the community patent in the coming months. the commission has just put forward a proposal on this subject. in addition, the flagship initiative on 'industrial policy in the era of globalisation', which is being prepared under the coordination of vice-president tajani and which we will present in october, will aim to improve the business environment, particularly for smes, and to support the development of a strong and sustainable industrial base, capable of facing global competition. in addition to these flagship continue on the path of reform. the commission communication adopted on 2 june underlines that all necessary measures will be proposed by spring 2011. but in fact the bulk of the work will be done under the belgian presidency. so this semester will be very busy, mr. prime minister. these measures are our response to the financial crisis and we have presented them in a coordinated and coherent manner. i am counting on the presidency to make as much progress as possible in the council, and on this parliament to give this area a high priority. in direct connection with this strategy for the principles for the next financial framework. the commission welcomes the belgian presidency's intention to carry out its evaluation in parallel with the work on the preparation of the 2011 budget. the mr president, i would first of all like to say to mr claeys that belgium has always solved its problems by political agreements and has always worked towards a stronger europe and stronger regions, closer to the people. the belgian presidency, as you have stressed, mr prime minister, is the first to implement the treaty of lisbon in full and to set the tone for future council presidencies by interpreting a new institutional agreement. the presidency must concentrate on the functioning of the council, since the european council has become a separate institution with a permanent president. the new treaty defines the powers of the council in exactly the same terms as those of the european parliament. the two bodies exercise jointly the legislative and budgetary functions. the treaty thus expressly establishes a bicameral system at union level. although there are important exceptions, codecision is now the general rule. i cannot help feeling that the council is still not sufficiently aware of this new development. this is evident, among other things, from the trialogue for the preparation of the 2011 budget. the belgian presidency can play an important role in this process of raising awareness. the presidency of the council should now function as the presidency of a parliamentary assembly. it should help to advance the union's legislative and budgetary agenda initiated by mr. prime minister, i hope by now you've understood that you have an opportunity to score a goal early in the game. i am, of course, referring to financial supervision. you have seen that there is an overwhelming majority in this house in favour of the establishment of a european policeman of the financial markets, whose actors are also very largely european. the council has so far defended a line which amounts, roughly, to maintaining the status quo, as if nothing had happened since 2008. only national egos seem to prevail on the model, unfortunately all too well known in our country, of wat wij zelf doen, doen we beter... if we want an operational agreement on 1 january 2011, the council will have to act, act strongly and act quickly. and if that means, as mr verhofstadt said, that the council must vote by qualified majority, then so be it. we did not adopt the treaty of lisbon to fall back immediately into the dictatorship of unanimity. given the circumstances, we have the opportunity to have a full-time rotating presidency, it has been said. so you have everything in your power to deny the prophets of evil fortune and make this presidency a success.
mr president, mr prime minister, in the last few days condolences have been pouring in. you have shot yourself in the foot, you have entered a presidency with a resigning government, the treaty of lisbon has, in any case, changed the game, the rotating presidencies have nothing to say and the last thing we hear is: 'thankfully, you have van rompuy'. to all these sympathetic messages, i reply that in the land of surrealism, magritte and the smurfs, nothing is impossible. mr. farage is a man of few imaginations. there you go! much more seriously, the work undertaken in recent months by the belgian federal structure and its regional governments will give this presidency the solidity it needs. there's no risk about it. the role of the rotating presidency is, after all, a kind of subsidiarity. let me explain: belgium must add value to what the council or the high representative is doing. that is where you have a real boulevard because, although i have heard herman van rompuy speak, time and again, about reassuring the financial markets, i have not heard him speak about reassuring the citizens. they're worried. they expect europe to provide a community response to the crisis in terms of financial regulation, job creation, green growth with projects, investments, supporting means and, in the end, a social breath and a european dynamism. i think that the strong belgian social traditions and your generous vision of cooperation and africa will inspire the european institutions, if not supply them with the saltpeter, which is, mr president, the favourite food of the smurfs.
president, mr minister, ladies and gentlemen, the circumstances in which the presidency is starting up are not ideal, but at the same time they are an opportunity to focus on the real task of a presidency under the new institutional rules. but i also see an opportunity to help steer the institutional debate, not only in our country but also in other eu countries. you know that the role of the nation states is increasingly being questioned by globalisation. the european parliament has been given more powers, and rightly so, which means that more powers can be transferred to flanders, wallonia, scotland, catalonia and others. this is a natural process which europe is well aware of. it is also quite logical that these nations should claim their place at the european table, since they play an important role in economic recovery. during this presidency, regional ministers will also chair council formations such as environment, sport, education, regional policy, and i hope that this can be a start to the necessary, more robust multilevel governance of the eu.
mr president, the crisis we are going through is the end of a model. while in recent months political priorities have been focused on the many anti-crisis measures to protect employment, to provide a long-delayed recovery and then to stabilise the euro area, we have arrived - it must be said - at a moment in our common history, albeit a delicate one, but above all crucial for our future. we have an incredible opportunity here, driven by circumstances, to dare to imagine solutions that were sometimes unthinkable just a few months ago, such as the famous concept of european economic governance. it is time to develop a real strategy for overcoming the crisis and to initiate structural reforms to build the post-crisis society. we must dare to take concrete measures in the budgetary, wage and tax fields. there are no 36 solutions: to guarantee real cooperation and the necessary efficiency, we need greater integration and unconditional support for the community method. this is the ambition i would like to see not only reaffirmed by belgium but, above all, translated into concrete action in a very urgent way.
i am convinced that the belgian presidency will surprise europe in a pleasant way, with some short-term successes, because the conditions are very good. it's been referred to. the swift file, the bank supervision and also the leverage fund file. it is logical, mr prime minister, that some eurosceptics and europolists, let us call them that, would not like to see this happen, of course, because they are terrified that europe will become stronger under the belgian presidency. it is particularly positive that you want to go above all for a stronger economy, stronger economic cooperation and above all for an action plan for more jobs. allow me, prime minister, to add one more point: i would like to thank the commissioner for his report and for the work he has done. it's a great binding agent. it is proven every day in south africa today. it is a link between regions, between countries and, above all, between groups of people, and i would say let us take the november council meeting on sport to make use of it and to put sport on the european agenda too. good luck to you, mr leterme!
the president, mr prime minister, i would first of all like to wish you and your team a very successful belgian presidency. a whole series of important priorities were reviewed during the debate. i would like to draw your attention to one very specific dossier which is of crucial importance for the european transport sector, for the further development of a good european transport infrastructure and for the achievement of our climate objectives, namely the eurovignette directive. it is a directive which we already discussed at first reading in march 2009 and which has been blocked in the council since then. however, it is important because we want to give member states the possibility of charging not only infrastructure costs to lorries using our roads, but also external costs such as noise, air pollution or congestion costs. this should lead to greater efficiency in the sector, help to reduce congestion and also provide member states with additional resources to reinvest in the transport system to make it more sustainable and contribute to additional economic growth. i would like to see your support for this directive.
the rapporteur. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would ask you to accept my request that my report be put to the vote by roll-call. i have informed my fellow shadow rapporteurs about this and i understand that they can support it. i believe that this will help to highlight the broad support for the report in the committee on economic and monetary affairs. thank you for your understanding.
in writing. i am very pleased with the vote on the draft regulation on novel foods. whether it is food products derived from cloned animals and their offspring or food products produced by nanotechnology, parliament has put the precautionary principle first. we do not have enough scientific knowledge and risk studies to allow these new ingredients in our food! i believe that we must support research and technological development. and i do not support the food fundamentalism that drives us to demand, some to demand, zero risk giving rise to sometimes excessive safety standards. in this case, however, i think we should be very careful, as european citizens asked in the 2008 eurobarometer survey. 65% of them said that they were mostly opposed to the consumption of cloned animals. finally, and not least, animal cloning is often associated with animal suffering. just remember the sheep dolly. in this respect, is our food supply so limited as to justify such practices?
in writing. the aim of this regulation is to define rules to regulate 'new foods' that may end up on the european consumer's plate. and the commission's proposal is far from satisfactory, mainly on two points. firstly, the health effects of nanotechnologies, which are too little known and therefore need to be subject to more specific control before they enter the market. this is already the case with cosmetics, but it is not the case with our food. secondly, food derived from cloned animals or their offspring, for which the commission and the council would not wish to grant specific treatment on the grounds that there is no evidence of a risk to the health of the consumer. it is simply unacceptable. even if we are not talking about the direct consumption of cloned animals, we want to remove these foods from the scope of the regulation and above all we want to see a public and transparent debate on this issue, which concerns both farmers and consumers. these are ethical questions which deserve to be asked in the name of a model of production and consumption which it is our duty to preserve.
in writing. once again, the european parliament rightly opposed the council. it is not possible, in the present state of our knowledge, to approve the placing on the market of cloned animals or even of their descendants. the council decides to ignore the recommendations of the european bioethics committee and efsa of 2008. there are too few scientific studies on the potential consequences for consumers and cloning procedures are not yet developed; many cloned animals die prematurely from malformations or deficiencies (cardiovascular, liver, respiratory, immune ...). in addition, cloning would reduce genetic diversity within the herd; it seriously threatens the european agricultural model, based on the quality of products, respect for the environment and strict animal welfare rules. we must ensure that consumers have meat of a quality that is beyond doubt. that is why my colleagues and i voted against the marketing of cloned animals or even their descendants.
in writing. - this directive on industrial emissions and integrated pollution prevention and control is incredibly important for the air quality of all europeans. the member states are now playing a very uneven game. some do their best, some don't at all. it is therefore regrettable that countries such as italy and the united kingdom have prevented the council from making the necessary improvements. i voted for the final text because it represents a clear tightening up, albeit with possibilities that can be spread over a period of 12 years. but the end result will be that coal-fired power stations in britain and italy, and elsewhere in europe, will also have to meet strict standards in order to ensure the health of our citizens in all the member states of europe. and that's what it's all about, in the end.
in writing. the plundering of tropical forests is a global scourge. and the european union, the second largest importer of timber after china, bears a heavy responsibility for the annual destruction of the world's 13 million hectares of forest. in addition to the ecological disaster, the lives of 350 million people and thousands of species are directly threatened. europe had to make a strong move. this is done with the adoption today by the european parliament of legislation which will oblige importers of timber to prove the legal origin of their goods. it thus puts an end to the system of impunity which has prevailed until now. a traceability system is now in place, from the concession where the tree was cut to the final delivery of the product. europe is on the right track. i welcome, for example, the recent signing of a partnership agreement with the republic of congo for the reform of its forestry sector. with one of the world's three major tropical rainforests, this friendly country is particularly vulnerable to illegal trade. the european union must now transform the test and encourage companies in the sector to increasingly turn to the import of wood certified by labels.
rapporteur. − mr president, first of all i want to thank elmar brok and roberto gualtieri, who were co-rapporteurs and who have also conducted the negotiations with mrs ashton, with the commission and with the council. the service has now been established, and i think that we can say that we have a good result that all the institutions can support and be proud of. i say this despite the remarks made yesterday by the french prime minister, mr fillon. he said that the service cannot go beyond certain red lines: the red lines of france. i have to say to mr fillon: ‘vous êtes trop tard’ – you are too late, because with this agreement on the service we went far beyond the red lines of france. this was no accident, but simply the full use and implementation of the lisbon treaty. it is foreseen in the lisbon treaty, which has been ratified by france, to pool foreign policy in the future in the union. what we need is not une bataille arrière-garde over the service, as the service is now there. what we need now is to use the service to develop a real eu foreign policy because – let us be honest – until now we have not had a real eu foreign policy at all. i remember when i was a member of the council in 2002 we spent all of 50 seconds on the iraq war, which was at that time the biggest foreign policy issue. europe at that time was not capable of discussing it, or of having a common position on it. we must not repeat that, certainly not in the multipolar world of today. continuing like that is not an option. it will be up to mrs ashton, and that is our message today. the service will need clear direction and will need leadership. the treaty has given mrs ashton many powers to do this. she has the right to convene a foreign affairs council, even at short notice. she has the power to take initiatives because she is also vice-president of the commission. she also has the responsibility to produce the general guidelines setting out the strategic interests of our union, and all of these responsibilities fall on her and her service. mrs ashton, allow me to give you one piece of advice at the end of this whole process of negotiation: develop the service as a community instrument. the union’s successes have been built on the tried and tested method which is the community method. only where the community method is applied have we made progress and had success. look, for example, at the single market. where it has not been applied we have had stagnation and even regression. look, for example, at the union’s response to the financial crisis. it is only through this method that we can succeed. it will be through the formulation of what i call a real ‘union interest’, free from member state interference, that a foreign policy of the union can be developed. it is by using the skills and expertise that you will have at your disposal that this real union interest will be identified. it will then be for mrs ashton to defend naturally this union interest because, in my opinion and the opinion of this parliament, what is good for the union is good for its member states and is also good for its citizens.
mr president, it is good that the european parliament is given a certain say in the european external action service. the control is now primarily carried out through the budget and the high representative for foreign affairs will be accountable to parliament for the rest. in fact, it is the most normal thing in the world in a democratic system or what a democratic system should be. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. we can now have a european diplomatic service, but if there is no common vision of foreign policy among the member states, then of course that service will remain largely empty shell. so the message is to be realistic, but i fear we are not all on the same page here, especially when i find an article on the website of our parliament entitled 'european parliament brings new diplomatic service closer to the citizens'. unfortunately, it does not explain exactly how this happens, but it is precisely because of this kind of hollow propaganda that more and more people in the european union are turning their backs on the eu.
the rapporteur. - mr president, i would first like to thank all the members who took part in the debate for their support for mr brok and mr gualtieri and for my own support, which has enabled us to achieve a result in these negotiations. i simply want to say that i do not understand how some people can claim that it is the nation states that should be exclusively responsible for foreign policy. what the hell century are they living in? as if terrorism stops at the borders of member states, of nations! the same goes for climate change: how can we approach climate change issues by arguing that it is the nation states that can discuss it, not europe? and for migration and migration flows, it's the same. i sometimes get the impression that many of my colleagues who are a little sceptical about this service are still living in the 19th century, when the nation states decided everything: the balance of power between the nations in europe. we live in a different world, mr. president. it is a multipolar world where europe has a say because otherwise we have nothing to say on these issues. there's a second element that kind of shocks me. i hear "it's going to cost the taxpayer money". on the contrary, mr president, this external action service will save money, because it will give several member states of the european union the opportunity to reduce their consular services in many countries in the world. so it's a tool to save money. and thirdly, i think this is a start. the external action service is the diplomatic service we need to implement the european union's external policy. i have always thought that we should start with a diplomatic service before we have a union foreign policy. without this service, we're not going to get anywhere with 27 different voices. we also know the next step, which is to work more on european defence. if we have european diplomacy without european defence, something is missing. so, mrs ashton, we already know the next workplace for the european institutions. it is a question of ensuring that there is a real european defence in the future.
president, ladies and gentlemen, mr minister, commissioner, commissioner, i thank the commission for the coherent and clear five-point plan presented today. i think this is very good and i trust that parliament will be happy to cooperate with it, particularly with regard to the moratorium, particularly with regard to updating and revising the legislation, particularly with regard to monitoring the controllers, particularly with regard to reviewing and strengthening the emergency plans, particularly with regard to greater transparency, for example by asking the member states to put all the results of their inspections on a website so that everyone can follow them. i would also like to address the belgian presidency. i would like to speak to mr magnette. i thank him for agreeing to work with us. but i have a tip for him, too. the ospar conference is being held this autumn in september. there will be a ministerial statement on safety at sea and i think that you, mr minister, should use that meeting to ensure that in that ministerial conference there are sufficient guarantees for an independent evaluation of what has happened, an independent and transparent evaluation of the existing regulations. i think you could do a lot of work there.

mr president, commissioner, i would also like to thank and congratulate our colleague, mr george lyon, on his excellent report, which is a positive first step towards a reform of the cap. the ambition of the new cap is that of a major european agricultural, food, social and environmental policy, not a market-oriented europe at all. we want it to be more legitimate in the public resources committed and accepted by the taxpayer, more equitable in the distribution of european funds between the 27 member states and between farmers, and more efficient in the proper use of public funds. new public mechanisms for regulating agricultural production will have to be put in place to ensure food security for consumers. we are in favour of a strong cap with the budget necessary to achieve its objectives. the report by mr lyon therefore rightly points to the emergence of new challenges facing the cap: climate change, water management, renewable energy, biodiversity, soil erosion, among others. the report calls for a more responsible, simpler cap, more effective in meeting the needs of european farmers, in the interests of consistency and sustainability... mr president, commissioner, at its meeting on 4 may 2010, the college of commissioners decided to relaunch negotiations for an association agreement between the european union and the mercosur countries. there is every reason to believe that if these negotiations lead to an agreement, european agricultural producers will face major problems, particularly in the beef, poultry, cereals and fruit and vegetable sectors. indirect adverse effects could also result for other sectors, such as pigmeat. i would like to warn against the unfair competition that this third integrated market in the world could bring. while europe is pioneering in health, hygiene, traceability, environmental and social standards, imposing very stringent conditions on european producers, the production of beef - especially brazilian beef, for example - still reveals veterinary and health deficiencies. by accepting imports from mercosur countries which do not meet european standards, we risk having to assume a heavy responsibility in defending the interests of our consumers and penalizing european producers by the practice of double standards. i would ask you, commissioner, to be very careful in this respect.
in writing. i voted in favour of the conclusion of the new eu-us agreement on the processing and transfer of financial data for the purposes of the terrorist financing monitoring programme, as i considered that the new proposal put forward by the commission offered increased guarantees to european citizens, in particular in terms of data protection. i believe that any transfer of european personal data to a third country for security purposes must respect procedural safeguards and the rights of the defence as well as comply with the applicable national and european data protection laws. a few months ago, the first version of the agreement was too vague and did not offer such guarantees. that's why we said no. today i am pleased that our proposals for improvement have been taken into account, namely, among other things, the establishment of a similar exchange system at european level and the guarantee that monitoring is entrusted to european officials who must be able to oppose the extraction of data on american soil.
author. madam president, behind the farai maguwu case lies the whole issue of diamond mining. zimbabwe is a voluntary member of the kimberley process, which allows signatories to sell diamonds on the international market as long as the trade does not finance armed conflict. but at the meeting in tel aviv on 20 and 21 june on the same kimberley process, the participants were unable to reach agreement on the following question: should not the issue of human rights also be included in the kimberley process? several international ngos have denounced human rights violations in zimbabwe's diamond mines, violations perpetrated by the security services. one of their informants was farai maguwu, director of a human rights research and development centre based in manicaland. maguwu was arrested on 3 june and has since been held in harsh conditions for allegedly endangering state security. this is clearly intolerable. the resolution calls for the immediate release of the convicted man, the 'demilitarisation' of mining, the inclusion of human rights clauses in the kimberley process and the redistribution of the diamond wealth to the people, for example through a sovereign fund dedicated to it. we also ask the commission and the council to be extremely vigilant on the issues of good governance and democracy in zimbabwe and in the dialogue, which is making little progress, with that country.
author. - madam president, in the case of maria lourdes afiuni in venezuela, is it the fight against corruption, which is the official version, or the persecution of a political opponent, which is the version of the new york times relayed by its correspondent, simon romero? the question certainly deserves to be asked, since such amalgamations are common in high-fat diets. but my group has withdrawn its signature from the compromise and is preparing to table a resolution on its own. i think you have tried to load the boat too much, ladies and gentlemen, and i do not want to bring the whole venezuelan regime down, which is what you are talking about, by means of this case, which i think should be clarified. what's this about? judge afiuni granted, through an exceptional procedure, relief to a banker who is accused of stealing $27 million from a state agency on the basis of a false import contract. she was arrested following this release and placed in custody. and it is true that the conditions of detention are absolutely appalling, as they are in all venezuelan prisons. but we're not doing a prison emergency here. so, that still doesn't make the judge a human rights activist - she never spoke out on this point. like you, i demand a fair and speedy trial with all the guarantees of the defence, the continuation of the fight against corruption within the judiciary, and the strict separation of politics and the judiciary. i grant you this, but i do not grant you the rest.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would first like to apologise to my colleague, mr luís alves, who was the fictitious rapporteur for our group, but who, because of lost luggage, was unable to join us in time and asked me to take his place, which i gladly do. i would like to emphasize the excellent report by our colleague, mr josé bové, in which we are advocating fairer incomes for farmers and a more transparent and efficient food chain in europe, and i am also pleased with the compromise we agreed on in the committee on agriculture. indeed, following the serious milk crisis we faced in 2009 and the causes of which still persist today, it was necessary to identify the toxic factors responsible for the long descent to hell of this sector, which is not the only one in this case. the commission has also proposed that the community should be able to provide the community with a financial contribution to the financing of the common agricultural policy. while we fully recognise the importance of a supply chain where producers and consumers are no longer the big losers at either end of the chain, and while we have managed to find balanced solutions, it seems that alde and the epp are revising some of the fundamental paragraphs of the compromise amendments negotiated before the parliamentary recess. unfortunately, my speech is too short to give details of the paragraphs which would be rejected in the vote in plenary. but if i have to mention just one, it will be paragraph 21, which considers it necessary to prohibit the sale of goods below cost at union level. i find it scandalous that the alde and epp groups are calling into question this paragraph, which is fundamental for our farmers. how can we allow our agricultural products to be sold to the retail sector and, even more flagrantly, to the processing sector? how can anyone, by rejecting paragraph 41 of the report, question the relevance of the creation of an independent global regulatory agency against speculation in food commodities? i could also quote paragraph 52, which is particularly close to my heart because i carried it in my group with my colleague csaba tabajdi. that paragraph considers that preferential treatment should be granted to producer groups, agricultural cooperatives and smes when awarding public contracts in the food supply chain and therefore invites the commission to propose measures to that end. it was also the subject of a compromise which now seems to be called into question by both the epp and the alde. at a time when millions of people around the world are suffering from malnutrition, hunger, and speculation is aggravating the financial problems of families - speculation on wheat stocks following the fires that hit russia this summer is currently a vivid example - would the agribusiness lobbies have been right about what seems obvious to us all? i wonder what bad play we're playing. will the epp and alde allow the logic of the processors and distributors to prevail over the defence of producers who are now under threat? at a time when food security and food sovereignty are under threat, it is irresponsible towards our citizens and farmers to reverse our original motion for a resolution, which is urgent for our agriculture, a fundamental part of our society, if it is necessary to recall it.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i propose that we throw a stone at iran. how can you accept this kind of barbaric treatment of a human being? did you know that in stoning, the man is buried up to his waist, the woman up to her chest, probably out of shame? the projectiles must be carefully selected, says the islamic penal code. average size is the rule. the projectiles must not be too large for one of them to kill, nor too small for them to be called pebbles. and the whole procedure should take a little less than half an hour. what about the reaction of hani ramadan, director of the islamic centre of geneva, who reassures us by saying that it is forbidden to insult the guilty and that after his death, we pray for him? what cynicism! what can we do in europe for the alleged perpetrator, mrs mohammadi? i propose the right of asylum in europe for sakineh mohammadi. i think that has not been proposed by anyone yet and that is something that we could add to the resolution.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the debates in the committees on employment and on budgets have resulted in a well-constructed draft report. i welcome the contributions of the rapporteur to the debate on the future of the european globalisation adjustment fund, with, for example, the necessary involvement of the social partners in the preparation of the dossiers or the attention that must be paid to the smes that are subcontractors of the large sites where redundancies take place. i also think it is appropriate that the prospect of an independent fund with payment and commitment appropriations be seriously considered in the future. it is simply a question of providing the social europe we wish to see with the appropriate means. some people don't hear it in this ear. thus, a division vote was called to remove this passage. it would be very damaging to the debate if the report did not mention all the ways in which the financing of these funds could be improved. i therefore call on the members to continue to improve this tool without excluding any possible outcome, by voting in favour of paragraph 16 as a whole on this issue.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, the state of the union means first of all looking back and learning the lessons of the past, and then looking to the future. let us be honest with ourselves and say that last year was a very difficult year for the union. it was a difficult year for the union because there were big problems, but little unity to solve those problems. greece was on the brink of collapse, the euro itself was under attack for months, and it took several months for the european governments to finally agree to save a member state and to save our common currency. it is therefore no surprise to me, nor, i think, to others here in parliament, that public confidence in europe has dropped dramatically. you can see this in the latest eurobarometer, which shows that less than half of our citizens think that their country’s membership is a good thing. trust in our institutions dropped last year to 42%. this is the state of the union today. that is alarming, but at the same time we have to recognise that it is not surprising. parliament urged european leaders many times to end the standstill on europe, to move forward, to quit protectionism and nationalism and to work on european solutions. that is also what the european citizens are asking for because, if you look at the same eurobarometer, you see that no less than 86% of the public want european economic governance. they believe that only the union can provide solutions to the financial and economic crisis. however, they do not see this happening today, and that is the reason for their disappointment in our union. i know that for the commission, too, it has been a difficult year, because in fact it has only been half a year. that is how long it has taken our capitals to realise that there is a new treaty and a new way of working, and also a new balance of power in the union. we can therefore say that today is the real start of the president of the commission’s second term. it is a second term that needs a new vision and also new answers. it is in any case time to change into a higher gear, and to realise important reforms now. from the point of view of our group, there are principally seven big, important reforms. the first thing to do is to finalise our response to the financial crisis. we have already produced legislation on capital requirements and on bonuses. we have the stress test, and last week we also reached an agreement on financial supervision, which is a good agreement, not least because leadership by the president of the european central bank guarantees a european approach to supervision. however, that does not mean that we are already there. far from it, i should say. we are not even half way. we still need the commission to come forward urgently with proposals on derivatives, on short-selling, on credit rating agencies, on banking resolution, on market abuse, on trading and on financial instruments. the second big task is that we need genuine european economic governance. last year showed that a common currency without a common economic policy simply does not make sense. that is a nonsensical situation – even worse, it is a dangerous situation. we cannot afford to be one union and at the same time to have 27 different economic strategies, as we do today. (applause)it is crystal clear that if we want the stability and growth pact and the new economic strategy to be a success, we need a serious carrot and stick approach, with proper sanctions. in my opinion – and i think in the opinion of the majority of this house – it is good that the council is thinking about this in connection with a task force, but that cannot replace the right of initiative of the commission, and the duty of the commission to come forward as fast as possible with a global legislative proposal on economic governance. (applause)i am pleased that you have announced that you will do this on 29 september. what happens in the council as well is not important. it is your task and it is your duty to do this on 29 september. that takes me to my third priority: the single market. we have an excellent report on this by mario monti, and our message is very simple: let us act on this report. because it is incomprehensible, for example, that today it still takes more than 40 hours to travel by train from central and eastern european countries to brussels, paris or amsterdam. how are these people supposed to feel connected to the european union? how can these markets be fully integrated? let us invest in trans-european networks. the fourth important priority, and maybe the most important message for today, concerns the budget and the new financial framework. let us be very clear about this. the aim of some national governments to reduce the budget by 20% or 30% is ridiculous at a time when what it needed is more european solutions. in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it tackles the problem of unequal distribution of profits in the food chain, with a particular focus on ensuring a fair income for farmers. the average income of eu-27 farmers fell by more than 12% in 2009 while consumers see stable or even higher prices for the final products they buy, reflecting the lack of transparency in price setting along the food chain and the increasing volatility of raw material prices. the commission is therefore calling for greater transparency in the food chain to put an end to the abusive practices of the supermarkets and the processing industry!
in writing. - i enthusiastically endorsed the report by josé bové on fair incomes for farmers and a better functioning food supply chain. there is a huge difference between the price the farmer gets for his products and what the consumer ultimately pays for them. where that difference remains is entirely unclear. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the price of food is set in a transparent manner, both for the farmer and the consumer. the report calls on the commission to identify the real costs of production of farmers and the price they receive for their products. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the food chain is not affected by the food crisis. farmers must not be the victims of opaque price agreements by supermarket chains. farmers' bargaining power is strengthened when it is clear how much profit intermediaries and supermarket chains make on a given product. moreover, a more transparent system can curb the abuse of power by supermarket chains to set prices. transparency and fair competition also make the food chain more sustainable. what is happening is that flanders is importing tomatoes from spain and spain is importing tomatoes from flanders. a better understanding of prices and profit margins can help to prevent these inefficient and environmentally harmful practices.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of my colleague josé bové's excellent report, through which we are advocating fairer incomes for our farmers and a more transparent and efficient food chain in europe. however, i regret the majority position of the epp and the alde, which, in returning to the compromise amendments negotiated several months ago, rejected certain fundamental paragraphs of this report. in fact, by rejecting in particular paragraph 52 which prohibits preferential treatment of producer groups, agricultural cooperatives and smes in the award of contracts, no doubt is left; it is now clear that the interests of the distribution and processing sectors have been privileged to the detriment of our producers.
in writing. i voted for this report because it is about the value of assessing social inclusion policies in the eu for women who belong to ethnic minority groups and identifying where they work and where there are problems to try to develop solutions. we live in a multicultural society that includes different cultural, ethnic and religious communities. to this end, it is useful that integration policies for third-country nationals include a broader gender perspective, which is necessary to ensure that the specific needs of women from minority ethnic groups are taken into account. a targeted approach to the social inclusion of women belonging to ethnic minorities is necessary to avoid multiple discrimination, stereotyping, stigmatisation and ethnic segregation.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because the ageing of our societies is too often perceived in negative terms, even though older people are an economic asset and an indispensable source of experience. in addition, older people are at higher risk of poverty than the general population: the poverty risk rate for people aged 65 and over was around 19% in the eu-27 in 2008, compared to 17% in 2000. i believe that a comprehensive and multidisciplinary approach to ageing is needed, as well as the creation of new opportunities, particularly in the area of products and services adapted to the needs of older people and informal family carers who care for dependent people. the commission should therefore be asked to propose an action plan containing these various measures by the end of 2011.
