in writing. budget 2011 vote between responsibility, bitterness and conviction. the s&d group and parliament took their part in voting in favour of this budget to avoid institutional deadlock and recourse to the provisional twelfths. the bitter truth is that codecision in budgetary matters is a match made in heaven and earth. despite the concessions made by parliament, some member states, who, unlike me, do not believe in the added value of the european budget, have remained inflexible. conviction: the future of the union depends on new own resources and a financial transaction tax. it is with the commission and its commitment to this matter that we shall achieve this fundamental objective. a ftt is necessary, but also a 2011 budget, so i abstained on the symbolic but irresponsible amendment tabled by the greens on this subject. the ftt is too important a subject to be played with in political strategies of introducing amendments which we are not the original authors of and providing for a zero budget line. this amendment, originally a socialist amendment, was intended to take this debate forward during the budgetary procedure, but to adopt it today would be to choose a union without a budget.
in writing. the european citizens' initiative will be a powerful tool for defining the issues to be put on the agenda of the european union. it was set up by the treaty of lisbon and will allow one million european citizens to ask the european commission to submit proposals in areas within its competence. this initiative thus gives the citizens of the union the right to be heard and at the same time grants them the same powers of political initiative as those already enjoyed by the council of ministers and the european parliament. the citizens' initiative will also have to encourage a wider cross-border debate, as it will have to be drawn up by people residing in several different member states. however, an initiative can only be implemented if certain requirements are met, whether they are administrative or respect for the fundamental values of the european union, to avoid this instrument being used for undemocratic purposes. these requirements guarantee its reliability and thus its effectiveness. it is also essential to ensure that the process is in line with union data protection requirements and fully transparent from start to finish.
in writing. participatory democracy makes its welcome entrance into europe with the adoption of this report on the citizens' initiative, one of the major developments of the lisbon treaty. one million european citizens will now be able to call on the commission to take a decision on a social issue, provided that it falls within european competence. this means that 1 million citizens - only 0.2% of the population of the european union! - are nationals of at least one quarter of the member states and are of voting age. signatures may be collected in writing, as well as on the internet, and must be verified. the authors will have to be identified: who they are, what they stand for, who they work for. this is potentially a giant step for the democratic legitimacy of europe. a step forward to bring the citizens of a europe they sometimes rightly or wrongly consider to be light years away from their daily concerns. but the risk of it being exploited by a few ngos or by one or another industrial sector is not insignificant. to be successful, the initiative will have to be genuinely citizen-driven. it will have to advance the debate, reconcile europe and its citizens, and contribute to the emergence of a european civil society.
in writing. - i voted in favour of establishing the rules of the european citizens' initiative. the ep has made sufficient progress in its negotiations with the council of ministers. the citizens' initiative gives citizens of the european union the opportunity to put a proposal on the european political agenda. once one million signatures have been collected, the european commission is obliged to examine the proposal and to explain why it is or is not an official legislative proposal. the european commission had set a threshold of at least nine different eu countries for signatories, but parliament succeeded in lowering it to seven. organisers who manage to collect enough signatures may also come to the european commission and the european parliament to explain their proposal in person. it is a pity that the european parliament has not been able to prevent 18 countries from requiring citizens to give their identity card number when signing a citizens' initiative. it scares people off. some will fear identity fraud. there are countries where the number of an identity card will not be asked. this creates legal inequality in the eu. it will now be up to the european commission to decide how seriously it will be taken by the european citizens.
in writing. the africa-eu strategic partnership is the only instrument of intercontinental relations of the european union. it marks a shift in relations between the eu and africa, which are becoming true partners, dealing as equals and whose political dialogue covers a wide variety of issues of common interest. it is no longer a relationship between givers and receivers. it is of strategic importance and needs to be revitalised and strengthened to meet the challenges ahead in africa and europe. i am thinking of the achievement of the mdgs, the food crisis, the economic and financial crisis, climate change and so on. the theme of the tripoli summit, 'investment, economic growth and job creation', recalls that peace and security, economic and political governance and respect for human rights are prerequisites for development. legal and judicial certainty for private investment is one of the keys to africa's economic and social development. there is no social, human and economic development without prosperity.
in writing. - the illegal extraction of minerals is already restricted by a us 'conflict minerals' law. the joint resolution on the future eu-africa strategy calls on the eu to do the same. minerals imported into the eu must be traceable. today, illegal exploitation is all too often at the root of civil wars and conflicts in the region, whereas this wealth can be the engine of sustainable economic development. actions in the areas of capacity building, good governance, infrastructure development and investment are crucial. a participatory policy, socially and environmentally responsible and beneficial to the population, is essential. agriculture is also a motor for development. the agricultural and fisheries sectors must therefore be strengthened in a sustainable way, especially for small farmers and fishermen. the debate on agricultural land and land ownership must be conducted in depth. the cancún agreement is vital to tackling poverty in africa, given the enormous potential of natural resources - sun, wind, rivers and tides - that african countries often have in abundance. the text of the resolution does not avoid the hot-button issues and was therefore approved.
in writing. the protection and promotion of human rights and fundamental freedoms are at the heart of democracy and the rule of law in the european union. the accession of the european union to the european convention on human rights will bring an additional mechanism to enforce human rights, namely the possibility of bringing an action before the european court of human rights. the european union institutions and the member states should also increase their efforts in the field of awareness raising so that citizens are aware of these fundamental rights and are thus better protected. it is also necessary to ensure better cooperation with international organisations concerned with the protection of fundamental rights and freedoms. it is important to develop a european union strategy on children's rights, through practical measures to combat abuse, sexual exploitation and child pornography, to promote safe use of the internet and to eliminate child labour and child poverty. the fight against trafficking in human beings - especially women and children - which is an intolerable form of slavery must remain a priority.
in writing. advertising is everywhere, on the streets, in newspapers, on television, on radio and on the internet... and whether we like it or not, it has a huge influence on consumer behavior. it's a means of communication. while the informative role of advertising is essential for the proper functioning of the internal market, the consumer has a right to be informed and protected. this is why the report on unfair commercial practices in advertising and on the problems associated with the development of new advertising practices and technologies is of great importance. advertising control is essential to keep it healthy and truthful. it is essential to strengthen european cooperation in this field, and it is also appropriate to encourage co-regulation. and online advertising is often aggressive and intrusive. it must be given serious thought.
in writing. until the crazy challenge of space solar becomes a reality and the whole world benefits from this almost inexhaustible electricity supply, the european union has no choice for its current energy policy but to multiply sources of supply and above all to play the energy efficiency card. this is a key priority of the european strategy for the next decade, as reiterated in the bendtsen report approved by a majority of meps this afternoon. i particularly welcome the adoption of the binding 20% energy savings target for 2020, which would save the eu some €100 billion. to achieve this, action will obviously have to be increased in areas as varied as energy services, transport (when will there be a framework for the standardisation of electric vehicles?) and buildings. the latter sector requires special attention when it is known that buildings are responsible for about 40% of the eu's energy consumption and about 36% of greenhouse gas emissions. there is considerable potential for energy savings in public buildings, for example, which would facilitate the transition to a sustainable and green economy.
in writing. − i am pleased that the european parliament has voted in favour of a mandatory target of 20% energy savings by 2020. projections indicate that europe will achieve only 11% energy savings with current policies. meanwhile, households waste a thousand euros a year on energy and we export as much as 350 billion euros of our european wealth to oil-rich countries every year. better insulated homes, more efficient energy transport and cleaner appliances mean lower energy bills for consumers and businesses. there are often barriers to taking these efficient measures, for example, due to the high investment costs of measures or uncertainty about the benefits. if the european ministers support the proposal, public authorities should do more to support energy-saving measures. measures that ultimately make money because the energy bill is lower. saving energy is the cheapest way to meet our climate goal and leads to 560 million tons less co2 emissions. so saving energy makes it even easier to increase our climate target. unfortunately, we are still hopelessly behind, and according to a recent report, european policy needs to be tripled to achieve 20% energy savings. that is why it is so important that this objective is binding on all eu member states.
for the greens/efa group madam president, i will be brief: if you look at the policies that are being implemented today, it is roughly 80% cut in expenditure and 20% new revenue, and i think i am still being generous in saying this. everyone knows very well that when you cut public spending by 80%, those who drink the first toast are the most vulnerable in our societies. i would therefore like to express my indignation at two contrasts: the first is the contrast i see between economic governance on the one hand and the eu 2020 strategy on the other. economic governance is hard, it is immediate, it is strict rules that are binding. the eu 2020 is, of course, a good idea - reducing poverty in particular - but it is soft, it is voluntary, we will do it if we want to, if we have time. there is no compulsion in all this. this is a contrast which i think is untenable and therefore incompatible with the spirit of article 9. the second contrast is between the question of economic governance on the one hand and the question of public spending on the other. if we want to cut public spending, we have to do it quickly, otherwise we are heading for disaster. we must reduce the debt in 20 years at most and, if possible, even faster, no impact studies, no real thinking. when it comes to generating new revenue - because a budget is both revenue and expenditure after all - taxes on financial transactions, taxes on energy, consolidated corporate tax base, we say "wait, we have to study, we have to think about the impact anyway, we have to not overdo it. let us think, take time, study... " and so, i don't really understand why on the one hand, you have to go fast and hard and not worry about the impact, and on the other hand, you go slow and think, and in the meantime, people are drinking. these contrasts, i think, show everyone that article 9 does not carry the same weight as the articles dealing with economic convergence in the union's treaties and i think this is a contrast we must resolve if we want to regain the confidence of our citizens.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, this report is a brick, it is a sum. it is almost exhaustive in terms of human rights and at the same time it has the faults of its qualities, that is to say, it is cumbersome, it is sometimes a little long to read but it is certainly not the fault of the rapporteur, but rather of the 423 amendments that he had to digest. so this is a feat for which mrs andrikiené should be congratulated. but the report also makes a clear demonstration that human rights, at the heart of european policies, are a complex, multifaceted subject and that, therefore, when the european parliament asks you, mrs ashton, a special rapporteur on human rights, a directorate-general for human rights, special training for the staff of the union's delegations abroad, and among that staff, a person who is very specifically responsible for monitoring human rights in the country in question, if parliament wants such structures, it is certainly not to bureaucratize the field, it is that there is really work to be done. the report does not ignore the serious problems and violations that continue to plague the world, but it is not a litany of horrors. it is right to highlight the efforts made, and does not hesitate to recommend actions and topics for dialogue. in conclusion, it reflects the importance and richness of the work done by the european union. the commission has developed a set of tools which can really help to promote democracy in the world. it has yet to convince its interlocutors that human rights are not a brake on growth and international trade, nor a drag in external relations, but on the contrary, an added value in terms of stability and prosperity. and you, madam high representative, have very well taken up the torch of human rights and left your mark at the end of this first year after the treaty of lisbon.

on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, i would first like to congratulate my colleague preda and his team on the remarkable work they have done, and also catherine ashton, the high representative, on the determination she has shown with the entire international community in monitoring these elections. for all of us who are absolutely fond of this extraordinary tool of election observation missions to promote democracy in a country, the fact that there is finally such a swift and determined follow-up to an attempt to illegitimately appropriate the election result warms our hearts and we hope that this can happen in other contexts if ever, alas, the situation requires it. now i would also like to say - because i have been told this many times - that ouattara is not europe's candidate. the whole international community has moved - and this is what is extraordinary - that is to say the african union, the economic community of west africa, the united nations, europe, etc., with one voice, and this too is to be marked with a white stone. now, on the question of mr ouattara wanting to take his activists to the streets, i am obviously a little bit more worried because i do not see peaceful solutions in the streets, in the context of the forces present. and i would like to return to a document which is currently being drafted in the acp and which was already discussed in kinshasa at the last acp meeting, where some of us were present. the title of the report is extraordinary: 'challenges for the future of democracy and respect for the constitutional order', and an african and a european representative are responsible for its drafting. and there's a lot of very legal stuff in that text and three pages that are devoted to power-sharing when one of the parties is trying to illegitimately seize power. these three pages of advice outline the course to follow to avoid a bloodbath. for it may not be in the streets, but perhaps through negotiations which would aim - once we have recognised who was the winner, of course, all the pressure is necessary - at some form of power sharing, that we may possibly be able to avoid a bloodbath. i recommend that you read this document. it is very informative and showed me that the african political culture may not know democracy, but certainly negotiations.
for the greens/efa group madam president, like those who have gone before me, i too, of course, point out the seriousness of the situation today, the acute political crisis but also the risk of clashes that could well take place in the coming hours. and so, in this respect, it is obvious that i welcome the unambiguous position taken by the entire international community and i think that beyond the need to give democracy its due, we have a responsibility not only to spare the ivorian people but also to give a signal, a clear signal that change in africa is possible. because, beyond the question of the ivorian people themselves, their security and the tensions that exist in that country today, which could reign in the coming days and months, the questions also concern the neighbouring countries. there will be elections in other neighbouring countries where the question of the alternation of an outgoing president against a new president could also arise. i think it is extremely important that in côte d'ivoire - not only because these people have already suffered enough, but also because it will serve as an example to neighbouring countries, and i am thinking of course of the congo, which will undoubtedly be the case in an election period next year, in 2011 - without any prejudice to the outcome, the question of democratic alternation can be played out peacefully, in a negotiated manner, with the support of the international community and in opposition, in any case, to all those who, on this occasion, would provoke bloodshed or, in any case, take the civilian population hostage. i welcome your statements on how to monitor, exert and maintain pressure in the coming weeks to preserve peace.
draft report by the committee on transport and tourism. - i would first like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this complex and very technical dossier, which is also very important for our future work. the old comitology procedure with the four different options was not unknown to our committee on transport and tourism and we have been familiar with how to deal with implementing decisions for a long time. but as regards the adjustments under the lisbon treaty, i would like to say that we support the compromises negotiated by the rapporteur with the council. however, there are two things we would like to draw your attention to. firstly, we want maximum information to be sent to parliament and maximum involvement of parliament, including in the preparation of decisions of the committee. secondly, i would like to stress the importance of a good approach to the transitional period, asking the commission to be generous in the transition from the comitology procedure existing in the acquis to the new delegated and implementing acts in favour of parliament, when a choice between both procedures may be open to discussion. this means not only the transposition but also the application of the current acquis pending transposition.
in writing. the european heritage label is, alongside other initiatives, an additional means of bringing the union closer to its citizens and of reinforcing the feeling of belonging to europe. it will also improve knowledge of europe's common history and heritage. the label will help to encourage multilingualism and dialogue between cultures, as well as the creation of networks to promote european heritage and stimulate economic attractiveness, in particular through cultural tourism. promoting the label enhances multicultural dialogue.
in writing. although not a member of the european economic area, switzerland has continued to express interest in closer cooperation with the european union in the field of education, training and youth. the conclusion of this agreement on switzerland's participation in the youth in action programme and the action programme in the field of lifelong learning is a very positive consequence. the concept of lifelong learning is essential to the competitiveness of the knowledge economy. it applies to all levels of education and training and concerns all stages of life and different forms of learning. exchanges, cooperation and mobility must be developed and strengthened. in addition, it is important to encourage the participation of young people in public life, in particular the most disadvantaged and disabled, and their spirit of initiative, enterprise and creativity in order to promote a sense of active european citizenship, develop solidarity and encourage tolerance.
in writing. yesterday, the european parliament awarded the sakharov prize for freedom of opinion to the absent cuban guillermo fariñas. an empty chair, because havana wouldn't have let him go home... a shocking, symbolic example, which viciously reminds us that the human rights situation in the world is not improving. the death penalty, which is very close to home in belarus, stoning and hanging in iran, female genital mutilation in somalia, gang rape in the democratic republic of congo, the murder of journalists in russia, 215 million children at work, arbitrary detentions, mock trials, censorship - all these barbarities require the european union to be firm and uncompromising. the report voted today calls for human rights to be at the heart of the european union's external action, in its development, defence, trade, fisheries, immigration and justice policies. the universality of these values must prevail over geopolitics, which was cynically revealed on 10 december last when 15 countries cowardly responded to china's explicit demand to boycott the award of the nobel peace prize to lu xiaobo of china.
in writing. − the report on the 2009 annual report on human rights in the world and the european union's human rights policy provides a near-complete overview of human rights problems in the world. the report also addresses a new set of human rights problems, such as the protection of human rights and the fight against terrorism. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. this is very important as the eeas as a new instrument can give us the opportunity to revise the eu's human rights policy and to defend human rights worldwide in a more coherent and effective way. i therefore support the proposal to appoint a high representative to coordinate the union's human rights policy with his or her department on a permanent basis. the message is clear: human rights must be at the heart of the european union's foreign policy. the commission has a long way to go but the ep is keeping its finger on the pulse. that is a very good thing.
in writing. we have a duty to enforce the electoral results of the ballot box, which express the will of the ivorian people. the results proclaimed by the constitutional council are contrary to the will of the ivorian people. we cannot accept this unconstitutional and undemocratic hold up. on 28 november 2010, alassane ouattara was declared the legitimate winner of the presidential election by the independent electoral commission and by almost the entire international community. it is urgent for the stability of the country and the whole of the subregion that the strategy of rotting implemented by laurent gbagbo be brought to an end as soon as possible.
in writing. animal welfare in general and in this case that of laying hens is a serious matter. the evidence is that not only did the european union legislate on the subject by means of a directive in 1999 which aims to ban battery-laying hens by 1 january 2012, but the transposition of this text in 2004 gave rise to a dispute between wallonians and flemings. it is essential in this debate to keep our heads and find a solution which takes into account both animal welfare and the economic interests involved. those who say that the rules of the community game must be respected, that unfair competition between farmers must be avoided and that a transitional period of 12 years is more than enough to pay off the investments are right. but those who oppose the rules, especially in this period of austerity, and who say that the changeover to enriched cages or to land-based farming will cost the poultry sector a lot of money, which does not benefit from direct aid from the cap, are also right. that is why i supported the three amendments tabled by the ppe group which take account of the daily reality of all european farmers.
author. - mr president, commissioner, exactly one year ago we voted in this house on a resolution on gays and lesbians and the bahati law in uganda. why come back a year later with almost the same resolution? because the problem is far from over. first, the evangelical movement the family, which was behind the bahati law, did not give up and we can still see the resurgence of this law in uganda. the second reason is that last october and november, the ugandan newspaper rolling stone made a name and shame and published the list of ugandan homosexuals who were immediately hunted down and raped and are still hiding today as outcasts. the ugandan constitutional court has ordered the newspaper to stop publishing, but the atmosphere is clearly deteriorating. this is a manhunt. third, only thirteen african countries recognize the rights of homosexuals. the rest of the countries have very different laws, but some of them - sudan, northern nigeria, mauritania - still punish homosexuals with death. so, and i'll end with this, there was a very recent statement, i would say a bit unilateral, from the acp countries, saying, "yes, we're bothering you a little bit about this homosexual problem, but it's our cultural aspects". no, these are human rights, these are universal values and we really want these universal values to be firmly defended by the commission and by the whole of the european union.
